Seven fields changed from `DEFAULT_RULES` (wider parallel street spacing 24→50m is the load-bearing one, plus longer segments, fewer pierces, gentler branch jitter, less plot-depth variance, a lower subdivision cap, one wall generation), each reasoned against `design/owner-references-2026-09-12/urban-town-plan-walled-market-town.jpg`. **Delivers the row's stated goal** (legible blocks instead of a shattered lane mesh, ~40–50% fewer blocks at pop 7 000/19 000 across 12 seeds × 3 sizes) but honestly does not reproduce the plan's radial arterials, suburbs, citadel or fields — that's the separate, larger "change the algorithm" row. Byte-identical on every existing golden (additive, no existing call path changed). **Verified independently**: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3507/0/36 exactly; the new market-town tests re-run in isolation; a windowed probe re-run independently reproduced the exact claimed edge counts (1121→821); real generated-town screenshots compared directly against the reference image confirm the block-grain match; `godot --headless --check-only` clean; `project.godot` unchanged. Commit `d287922`. **A real, reproducible hang bug in existing shipped code was found as a side effect — see the new row below, not fixed here.** |
| ~~**`build_parcels`'s frontage-width loop can hang**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified)** | `cartalith-urban/src/blocks.rs`; `RC_ENGINE_CHANGES.md` §6r.5 | medium | **Fixed by porting the reference's own `PARCEL_GRANT_MAX_SPIN = 4096`** (§6r.5's cited value): counts consecutive frontage-grant passes that place nothing, resets on any real placement, stops granting on that edge once hit — the existing post-loop stretch widens already-placed grants to fill it, no new fallback invented. **Bound chosen by measurement**: across 60 real towns, the worst unbounded run was 159 367 spins (reference's own figure: 172 644); 16 384 and 65 536 also terminate every case at the same cost, so the reference's own 4096 was kept. The 21 of 60 towns that never reach the cap are byte-identical under it (hash, count, every parcel's geometry) — proving the bound doesn't touch normal generation. **Before/after timing**: the previously-hanging cases (pop 7 000 at variance 0.15, ~43s; the shipped "Planned Grid" preset's pop 4 000 at variance 0.10, >100s on every seed tested) now both finish in ~100ms. **Golden re-baseline, pre-authorized**: 20 of 29 `generate/golden.rs` cases move (67 of 31 845 parcels by count, 0.21%, close to the reference's own 0.51% for its equivalent fix) — only parcel-derived fields, nothing about streets/blocks/plaza/walls/forts/markets/bridges; 9 cases including all 6 Venus plans are byte-identical, the control. `districts/golden.rs` (calls `build_parcels` directly, needed the same re-baseline though outside the row's named scope): 22 of 36 scenarios move, only churchyard parcel IDs renumber — the worship site itself is bit-identical on every one. A new test hangs (not fails) if the cap is ever removed, a deliberate choice so a regression is loud. **Verified independently**: full diff read; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3510/0/36 exactly; golden diffs confirmed at exactly 20 hash changes (generate) and 22 parcel_count changes (districts), matching the disclosed counts precisely. Commit `86ccf73`. |
| ~~**A citadel enclosure straddling the town wall**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified), commit `9467a23`** | `URBAN_MORPHOLOGY_SCOPE.md` | large | **Ruling I, 2026-09-12 — new engine work, no reference ancestor.** New `cartalith-urban/src/citadel.rs`: a walled sub-enclosure astride the curtain (90-120×80-100m), towers at each corner plus every curtain-wall crossing, a keep against the outer face (new geometry, not the reused "Castle keep" civic hall — that's framed off the market square, and moving it would move every civic hall), an open court, an inner gate with its own approach street. Site selection requires the highest ground along the curtain, dry, clear of every gate by half-width+40m, crossing no primary road, genuinely astride, with a reachable junction — no qualifying spot means no citadel, a normal outcome. Bastioned towns get none. **Gated per Ruling AC (2026-09-23): `pop_target >= CITADEL_MIN_POP` (10 000) on the organic plan only** — an existing crate boundary (`build_civic`'s "Town hall" naming, wallside's third faubourg run), so Capital/Metropolis qualify and City does not: size, not faction seat. **The ruling's own open question (circuit-growth) answered conservatively and disclosed**: `build_citadel` only reads `wall_state`, never writes it, so the ring/`building_intramural`/rampart band are unaffected; `Town::citadel` is its own field, not folded into `Town::wall`. Wired through `UrbanLayout::citadel`, `urban_bridge.rs`'s `citadel_*` keys, and `urban_layout_draw.gd::_draw_citadel`. **Golden re-baseline: exactly 1 of 29 cases qualifies and moves** (`coastHarbourChain`, pop 12 000, curtain), disclosed old→new; 28 unmoved including six more at or above the tier (4 bastioned, 2 radial) — a new test pins the set. **Built in an isolated worktree concurrently with the radial-branch commit above (both touch `cartalith-urban`), merged by hand** (adjacent doc-comment/struct insertion points in `generate.rs`, two separate golden cases — no true logical conflict); full workspace suite re-run after the merge, 3563/0/37, exactly 3559+4 new citadel tests. **Verified**: a geometry test across 40 real towns (astride property, tower/gate/keep/court placement, no primary crossing, cleared lots); 11-mutant sweep, 9 killed, 2 named defensive-guard survivors; windowed probe (a settlement pushed to 15 000 pop) — no citadel keys below the tier, real keys above, 3 162/0 and 36 290/0 changed-pixel ratios inside vs. outside the citadel's screen box; real before/after screenshots viewed directly post-merge confirming the citadel astride the curtain in a real generated town. |
| ~~**A menu to set a settlement's city type and regenerate that one settlement**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified)** | `URBAN_MORPHOLOGY_SCOPE.md` | large | **Ruling J, 2026-09-12, in the owner's words:** *"Let's create a menu to modify city types and be able to regenerate a specific settlement at will."* **What "city type" turned out to mean, checked at the symbols, not invented**: a settlement's **culture profile** (`cartalith_urban::CULTURE_PROFILES` — `medieval`/`venus`; `run_layout` hard-coded `None`) and its **rule set** (`DEFAULT_RULES` or the `market_town` preset; `settlement_layout_with` already took `rules: Option<&Rules>` but only ever received the world-level active rules). Size tier stays in the Place Editor's Classification (it moves territory and roads); the GDScript-only `SettlementTypeStore` bundle is a separate thing. New "Town plan" section in `city_viewer_window.gd`: Culture, Rule set, Walls (the existing `civ_edit_settlement` walls field), a Regenerate button and an Original button. **Regenerate is a new stored `variant`** (not a cache-bust of a deterministic function, which would draw the same town) — `um_place_context_with` mixes it into the position-derived seed only when set (`seed ^ v.wrapping_mul(0x9E37_79B9)`), so every other input (site, water, terrain, roads) stays that settlement's own. New engine surface, all additive — unset reproduces today's call exactly: `PlaceOverrides::{culture, variant}`, `civ_set_town_plan` (all-or-nothing, validates before writing, deliberately does not set `civ_dirty` since a town plan moves no territory/road/trade), `urban_town_plan_options`, `RULES_PRESETS` (single source of truth `rules_preset` now reads from). `map_overlay.gd::forget_urban_layout(index)` drops exactly one cached town so Regenerate redraws only it. **A real layout bug found and fixed while building**: the Town plan column sat in a horizontal-scroll-disabled `ScrollContainer`, which passed its contents' minimum width up and squeezed the column from 676px to 436px (the `MISTAKES.md` disabled-axis trap) — `_fit_column` restores it, probe-asserted. **Verified independently**: full diff read at every symbol across 8 `.rs`/`.gd` files (the two touching `cartalith-godot/src/lib.rs` cleanly split from the concurrently-landed tier-promote/demote batch — two disjoint hunks 14 000+ lines apart, confirmed by `git diff` hunk headers, no overlap); `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3603→3629 exactly (this row's 3 new tests — culture/variant override reaches the layout, `set_town_plan` validates before writing anything, an unset town plan writes no archive key — plus the concurrent batch's 23); mutation-tested, both wires killed (`culture: ctx.culture.clone()` → `None`, the seed-mix removed); the real windowed probe (`_townplan_probe.gd`/`.tscn`, 40-settlement generated world) confirms byte-identical output with every override unset, all 39 *other* settlements' layouts and stored overrides untouched after regenerating one (`settlements()`/`roads()`/`trade_balances()`/`stale_stages()` compared directly, not assumed), and the panel round-trips its state on close/reopen; real before/after screenshots viewed directly show the same settlement drawn as its existing organic medieval plan, then as a genuinely different Venus radial plan, on demand. `godot --headless --check-only` clean on all touched `.gd` files. **Disclosed, not built**: Venus doesn't auto-disable walls the way the reference's UI does (left to the Walls control, tooltip says so); no phone probe (the column is full-width there, so the measured squeeze doesn't apply, but untested); the Generation rules window's world-level edits still don't invalidate the map's town cache (pre-existing, unchanged — only this new path invalidates it). |

adapter — recorded its blocker as *"needs a block-2 capture harness that can run
`_um*` inside the host's full civ scope; the existing harness slices block 4
only"*. That premise is disproved by a running counter-example:
`cartalith-native/tools/um_block2_capture.js` drives the unmodified reference
under Node and `crates/cartalith-civ/tests/golden_parity_urban_adapter.rs` now
holds the extracted fixtures. Mutation matrix **22/22 killed**, and an
independent verifier confirmed the fixtures are genuinely reference-extracted
rather than replayed from the Rust port.

Golden re-baseline: 3 of 29 cases move (`venusRadial`, `venusSmall`, `venusTinyCanal`), disclosed old→new; 26 unmoved. Ribbon suburbs from `grow` deliberately NOT ported (would have been the macro-structure reshape Ruling AA rejected); most radial towns have no land gate, so the faubourg falls back to a wall starting point, disclosed in the golden header. **Disclosed follow-ups, not built**: adding gate roads to the radial plan (a separate change); Venus towns are unreachable from the shipping app today (`urban_adapter` never passes a culture), so this only shows through `cartalith_urban::generate` directly or the new `town_json` example. Golden re-baseline: 21 of 29 cases move, disclosed old→new; 8 unmoved (6 Venus + 2 organic towns with no dense-enough outer block) are the control. Caught and fixed a real bug in `geom::inset_poly` along the way (an offset past half-width silently returned the polygon's mirror rather than failing). **Two disclosed follow-ups, not resolved, worth an owner call**: the agrarian fringe loses its orchard trees on converted blocks (ring lots are never paddocks); engine blocks run smaller than the reference's, so ring lots fan out wedge-shaped on sharp corners rather than square to the street. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3563→3581 across both commits combined (shared tree with two other concurrent batches); each batch's own contribution independently isolated and confirmed in its own commit message. **Verified independently for both commits**: full diff review at the symbol, golden headers checked for exact old→new disclosure, real before/after screenshots viewed directly (faubourg clusters; courtyard rings matching the reference's SE-suburb blocks), windowed probes re-confirmed. |

**The pass found two real port bugs the 11 synthetic-field unit tests could not
see** — which is the argument for golden-verification, stated concretely:

- `slope_at` used `f64::hypot` where the reference uses `Math.hypot`. This is
  the exact V8-libm divergence `CLAUDE.md` warns about and `geom::js_hypot`
  exists for.
- `um_site_profile` clamped the resource-context centre where the reference does
  not.

A third defect was in the fixture itself (`background_pots` never mirrored the
harness's one iron deposit), caught before it could be committed as truth.

*The "built and uncommitted — `git log 4ec07f5..HEAD` returns nothing" note this
section carried is history: `cff1edc` landed the downstream wiring.*

> **Before executing any ruling that says "add `cartalith-urban` as a dependency
> of `cartalith-godot`": the substance is already done.** `urban_bridge.rs`
> ("the first consumer `cartalith-urban` has ever had") reaches the crate
> through `cartalith_civ::urban_adapter`, which is the layering
> `cartalith-civ/Cargo.toml:18-22` explicitly defends. The "zero consumers"
> sentence `URBAN_MORPHOLOGY_SCOPE.md` used to carry (removed in its 2026-09-24
> cleanup, `c4c930c`) was a **quotation of what
> `PARITY_AUDIT.md` §3.4 found before milestone 17a**, and the same paragraph
> describes closing it. What is missing is the generator stages above, not the
> Cargo edge; adding a direct edge would buy nothing and violate the layering.

### 2.3 Civilisation, economy and journeys

| Item | Owns it | Size | Next step |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**Territory lasso — assign an area to a faction by drawing a polygon round it**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (filed and closed in the same pass; awaiting independent verification)** | owner request, 2026-09-23 | small | **The owner, verbatim:** *"for labelling regions etc, a user must be able to select the region in question. I was thinking to use the polygonal selector tool would be a good one the assign a certain area under a faction/dominion/province manually."* **A new INPUT for the existing Territory backend, not a new backend.** New CIVIL tool `territory_lasso` ("Territory lasso (⇧T)", glyph `tool_lasso`), beside — not replacing — the rectangle Region select, which is untouched. Interaction follows Measure's Area-ring convention (`global_tools.gd`): click adds a vertex, the ring draws implicitly closed from three (`tool_overlay.path_preview`), ⌫ drops the last, Esc clears and stays armed, arming any other tool drops a half-drawn ring; ✓ Commit / Discard on the options row, whose Faction and Add/Subtract are the brush's own `_territory_faction`/`_territory_subtract` (one choice, two input methods). Engine side: `civ_tools_bridge::polygon_cell_mask` (even-odd scanline over cell centres `(x+0.5, y+0.5)`, the overlay's own cell convention) and `CivTools::paint_polygon`, bound as `WorldGen::civ_territory_paint_polygon`. The ring goes into the brush's `territory_draft` as **one** `PaintStamp` — a disc covering the ring's bounding box, gated by the inverted mask through the stamp's existing `mask` field — so `civ_territory_commit`/`_discard`/`rebase` and subtract-restores-the-base are the brush's, unmodified. One stamp rather than one per cell because `PassBuffer::push` snapshots the whole entry stack each push (quadratic in area). No golden path reads any of it. **Verified:** 9 new unit tests (`lasso_*` in `civ_tools_bridge.rs`: exact cells of a square, clipping off-grid, sub-cell rings, degenerate/NaN/empty rings, a vertex exactly on a centre row, a self-intersecting bow-tie, commit-inside-only then subtract-restores-the-base, a long thin ring reached end to end by the one stamp, discard); 7 mutants, 6 killed, the survivor (`len() < 3` → `< 2`) equivalent — a two-point ring self-cancels in the scanline. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` 3694 → 3703 passed, 0 failed, 40 ignored. Windowed probe `_terrlasso_probe.tscn` (seed 131313, 256×192): arms by clicking the palette button, places the ring with real mouse clicks pushed through the viewport into `map_overlay.gd::_gui_input`, ⌫ via a real key, faction and Commit through the options row — 3 713 ring cells (2 134 previously another faction's) all become faction 3, **0** pixels changed outside the ring against an independent `Geometry2D.is_point_in_polygon` oracle, claimed count +3 713 exactly; Esc/Discard change nothing; a Subtract ring over the same polygon restores the pre-edit territory image **byte for byte**. `_toolstrip_probe` (click + ⇧T + lit, options-row width 1181 < 1600) and `_phonemore_act_probe` (phone row arms it) extended and green. **Disclosed, not built:** (1) **Province assignment** — provinces are real (`CivData::provinces`/`province_list`, `get_provinces`) but *derived*: `civ_generate_provinces` recomputes them from territory + settlements inside `compute_civilisation`, with no paint layer, no hand-assignable state and the invariant that a province never crosses a faction boundary. A manual province lasso needs that state designed first — follow-up row below. "Dominion" is read as the owner's word for a faction's area, not a third entity. (2) Committed territory is still ledger-recorded and not reversible (ED-02, `civ_territory_commit`'s own comment); "undo" is the Subtract ring, which restores the computed base. (3) Like the brush, the lasso is ungated — a ring over open water claims it. (4) Like the brush, a commit does not regenerate provinces/trade (the existing status hint says so). |

All five of §4.3's required tests pass with real measured numbers — zero differing bytes at every band size tested including a wider-radius case, no boundary step, non-vacuous negative controls (forcing the apron to 0 diverges by 42 120 bytes, proving the apron is what makes the identity hold), exact plan arithmetic 2K-32K. Four mutation tests each caught a real defect. One honest caveat disclosed: §4.2's f64-exactness proof is measured exact at test size but not strictly proven at 32K's extreme window (~55 bits needed against f64's 53) — a byte would only flip on a coincident u8-rounding edge. Verified independently: `ExportBandPlan`/`bake_export_band` confirmed present at the symbol, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` re-run (164/3485/0/34, matching the agent's own math exactly against the concurrently-landed QuadTree retirement). Commit `de3c95e`. **E2 (the streaming writer) CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified)**: new `export_stream.rs` pulls each band from `bake_export_band` lazily, in order, and writes it through a `BufWriter` before dropping it — the finished raster is never held whole. PNG and BigTIFF both land. **Measured**: 32K PNG peaks at ~1.33 GB against the monolithic path's ~15.2–15.3 GB, tracking band height at ~21.9 bytes/rendered-band-pixel. **Round-trip verified exactly to the milestone's own stated bar** — decoded with a reader sharing no code with either encoder, every byte, several band geometries including a short final band; cross-checked externally against Pillow/libtiff at the full 32768×20976 scale, 0 differing pixels. **Two real findings, not guessed**: (1) `EXPORT_SCOPE.md` §6.1's own documented BigTIFF recipe (`new_big()`→`rows_per_strip()`→`write_strip()`) writes a **corrupt file** in `tiff` 0.11.3 — only the whole-image path actually enables compression; worked around by applying the predictor and the crate's public `Deflate` compressor directly through `DirectoryEncoder`. §6.1 needs correcting separately, not done here. (2) the shipped PNG export has always used `Fast` compression, not `Balanced` as an earlier doc comment assumed — caught because a first attempt at `Balanced` came out 17% smaller than the shipped path; the streaming writer now matches `Fast` so `export_raster.rs`'s fitted size/timing model still describes it. **Not wired into the shipped export menu yet** (that's E3) — a new `#[func]` would be one line. `tiff` 0.11.3 added as a new dependency, confirmed MIT-licensed at the registry cache, `default-features=false` keeping only `deflate`; `Cargo.lock` gained exactly two packages (`tiff`, `quick-error`), confirming §6.2's predicted trimmed resolution. **Verified independently**: full diff read including the BigTIFF workaround itself; `tiff`'s MIT license confirmed directly; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3516/0/37 exactly, purely additive. Commit `fc7db2b`. **E3 (the options struct) CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified)**: one `ExportOptions {width, format, style, content}` dictionary (`export_options.rs`) replaces the "fifteen `#[func]` parameters" §7 named — unknown keys/looks/ramps/tunables/formats, a look+preset together, a missing preset file, or a tier with settlements off are all refused, not ignored. **`style` layers over `appearance()` without mutating session state structurally, not just behaviorally**: `appearance_rebased(&self, …)` takes `&self`, so it cannot write to `WorldGen` at all — verified independently at the symbol. `content` is §5's explicit settlement tier; overlays beyond terrain+rivers need E4 (doesn't exist) and are refused outright, never faked. Two new `#[func]`s (`export_image`, `export_image_estimate`), not wired into any menu — that's E5. `export_raster_estimate` gains real `bands`/`band_rows`/`apron_rows`/`band_peak_bytes`/`band_affordable` figures, checked against hand-worked-out numbers in advance: 16K is 5 bands/2294 rows/apron 164, 32K is 33 bands/655 rows/apron 328, both peaking at exactly the existing 8K monolithic figure. **Divergent refusal policy flagged and RULED ON 2026-09-23**: on a platform reporting no free memory, the new `export_image` path allows 16K/32K (its real banded cost never exceeds the 8K monolithic figure) while `export_raster_png` still refuses anything above 8K there. **Owner: keep `export_raster_png` as-is** — its caution is correct, not a bug, because it is NOT banded and its real cost at 32K genuinely is ~15 GB; the coordinating session's own first framing of this question was imprecise (it proposed "relaxing" the old path to match, which would have let a real ~15 GB allocation proceed on a platform that couldn't report free memory — caught and corrected before any code changed). No code change needed; the two paths' different thresholds correctly reflect their different real costs. **Verified independently**: full diff read including confirming the `&self` non-mutation guarantee at the symbol; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3525/0/37 exactly, purely additive (9 new tests); no bug found in or contract changed for E1/E2. Commit `ce2c71d`. **E4 (the overlay session) SCOPED 2026-09-23, not built** — real measurement, not a guess, found genuine risks a naive port would have hit: (1) a 32768px-wide `SubViewport` returns a null image on this Godot version — a full-width 32K band cannot be one viewport, the overlay must tile on both axes (Android's limit is unmeasured and likely lower). (2) `SubViewport` readback is **premultiplied** alpha, not straight — compositing with the straight-alpha formula would darken every antialiased edge; measured exactly (`[128,128,128,128]` for a 50%-white rect). (3) the export's pixel→grid mapping is corner-aligned (`bake_rect`'s own `sx=(gw-1)/(W-1)`) while the live overlay's is texel-centred — a naive "control size W, zoom 1" camera would compress the overlay by ≈8px at 32K's edges; the real fix is a derived per-axis affine on a parent-node camera (not `SubViewport.canvas_transform`, which the urban-layout culler reads around). (4) symbol-size semantics are split three ways inside `map_overlay.gd` (pins scale with fitted map width, way/label widths are constant screen-px, glyph rasterisation caps at 256px) — genuinely gates the whole approach, filed as an owner question and answered 2026-09-23: **uniform magnification** (symbols stay proportionally sized on a giant export, like a real poster) — the bigger-lift option, needs new symbol-scale drawing code in `map_overlay.gd`, not the recommended v1 screen-pixel-constant shortcut. (5) state-copy is a silent-divergence risk — missing one setter makes the export differ from the screen with nothing failing; the plan requires deriving the full setter list from every real caller and proving parity by diffing the live and export overlays under an identical camera, not just eyeballing it. **Sequenced into 4 independently-verified batches** (Rust session core → one-tile registration → many-tiles/many-bands end to end → wiring+real-size measurement+docs), matching this session's own batch discipline for large, architecturally novel work. **Five more owner questions remain, gating batches B-D** (v1 overlay content scope, LOD rule for labels/ways/urban layouts, whether rivers draw the vector stroke or the baked ink when overlays are on, which settlements/filters apply, whether a 1-2s per-band UI freeze is acceptable for v1) — not yet asked. **Stale items found in passing**: `STATUS.md`'s export group said "shelved/not started" for all five rows (un-shelved 2026-09-06). **Fixed 2026-09-23**: E1-E3 are `done`, E4 `partial`, E5 `not started`, each re-verified at its symbol. `map_overlay.gd`'s header comment describes an obsolete scene relationship, which is still not fixed. **E4 Batch A landed 2026-09-23, commit `34db87f`** (Rust core, no GDScript wiring yet): `BandSink` (`export_stream.rs`, a writer thread over a `sync_channel(1)` feeding the existing `write_bands`); `ExportSnapshot`/`ExportSessionCore` (new `export_session.rs`, `Send+Sync` and Godot-free, matching `LodSnapshot`'s own shape, state machine `Idle → Open → Finished\|Aborted`); `composite_premul_over` (the premultiplied-alpha formula a `SubViewport`'s `transparent_bg` readback needs, confirmed against a real `[128,128,128,128]` readback of a 50%-white rect); five new `#[func]`s (`export_session_begin/_submit_tile/_finish/_abort/_state`); `export_image`'s pre-render checks factored into a shared `prepare_export`, used by both it and `export_session_begin` (behaviour-preserving, not a new check). Session output proven immune to world regeneration mid-session (renders from its own snapshot) via `_exportsession_probe.gd/.tscn`. A concurrent-agent `git stash` swept this batch's uncommitted `lib.rs` hunks together with the river-colour batch's; the pop restored both, independently confirmed by symbol-level diff review, not just the agents' own reports. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 3542/0/37. **Still remaining: E4 batches B (one-tile registration), C (many-tiles/bands end-to-end) and D (wiring, measurement, docs) — no GDScript caller of the new session `#[func]`s exists yet — and E5 (the dialog)**. **The render-once decision was NOT reversed**; what depends on it is enumerated in §3 |

That is a real, correct, already-shipped feature reading the same `TimelineSnapshot` source SP-3 needs — but it answers "who ruled this place, when," not "what was its population/tier/ruins/fortified, when," which is what SP-3's own spec text says. **So: population/tier ARE capturable but nothing reads them into a trajectory.** `TimelineSnapshot.settlements: Vec<NamedSettlement>` (`lib.rs` ~5750-5766) already carries `pop: u32` and `placement.kind: SettlementKind` (`lib.rs` ~4464-4472, the tier enum) for every recorded year — an accessor of `civ_settlement_ownership_periods`'s own shape (walk each snapshot, this tid, emit `(year, pop, kind)`) would close this leg. Additive, read-only, no golden risk, same pattern already proven — the smallest of the four pieces and the only one that's shovel-ready as stated. **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified).** `cartalith_civ::timeline::civ_settlement_population_trajectory(timeline, tid) -> Vec<PopulationPoint>` (`timeline.rs`, next to `civ_settlement_ownership_periods` at ~1936, the new function at ~2008) walks every recorded `TimelineSnapshot` in year order and returns one `{year, pop, kind}` per year the settlement was actually present in — deliberately **flat, not collapsed into tier-change spans** the way ownership collapses same-faction years into one span: population moves almost every recorded year (generation's own per-settlement RNG variance, then every later growth/famine/war/recovery step), so a span-collapsed shape would throw away exactly the numbers a trajectory needs, unlike ownership's categorical faction — reasoning stated in the function's own doc comment. Three new unit tests in `timeline.rs`'s own test module, same fixture shape as `civ_settlement_ownership_periods`'s own suite: `population_trajectory_is_empty_for_an_empty_timeline_or_the_unassigned_sentinel`, `population_trajectory_returns_every_recorded_year_flat_not_collapsed_into_spans` (a tier change at year 100 alongside population changing every recorded year), `population_trajectory_skips_a_year_the_settlement_is_absent_from_rather_than_carrying_forward`. Wired to Godot as `WorldGen::civ_settlement_population_trajectory` (`cartalith-godot/src/lib.rs`, the `#[func]` beside `civ_settlement_ownership_periods`'s own), marshalling `kind` through the existing `journey_bridge::settlement_kind_key` vocabulary — no new one invented. New windowed probe `_peptraj_probe.gd`/`.tscn` builds a real 4-year timeline (`civ_add_year` staging, same technique `_pepolitical_probe.gd` uses) on a real generated+simulated world with both a population change and a tier change (capital → town, via `civ_edit_settlement`), cross-checks the bridge's own `civ_settlement_population_trajectory` output against the real drawn `Label` text in the Political History tab: 22/22 checks pass, exit 0. `godot --headless --check-only` clean on both touched `.gd` files (`place_editor_window.gd`, `engine_bridge.gd`) and the new probe script. `.dll` rebuilt after both `.rs` edits (confirmed by mtime, not assumed). **`ruins`/`fortified` are a real, pre-existing, already-disclosed gap, not new work to discover.** `timeline_bridge.rs`'s own top-of-file doc comment (~22-48) states outright that `CollapsePlace.fortified`/`.ruins` (`timeline.rs` `CollapsePlace` ~958-975) never reach `NamedSettlement` or `TimelineSnapshot` — `collapse_place_from_named_settlement` (`timeline_bridge.rs` ~201-212) hardcodes both `false` on the way in, so they exist only transiently mid-simulation and are gone by the time a step's result is written back to a stored snapshot. Closing this needs a real decision: either widen `NamedSettlement` (the doc comment's own suggestion, but it warns this "would ripple into every other subsystem that constructs one" — `civ_tools_bridge.rs`, `render.rs`, all of Phase 2's placement pipeline), or carry `fortified`/`ruins` as a parallel per-`(tid, year)` side table written only by the collapse/recovery step path, leaving `NamedSettlement` untouched. Not decided here — a real design choice for whoever builds it, not an owner ruling, but state it rather than guess it. **`ruins`/`fortified` — CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified).** Builder's call, measured not guessed: `git grep -n "NamedSettlement {" -- crates` gave 59 hits across 22 files against 15 hits across 3 files for `TimelineSnapshot {` — only one of those 59 (`named_settlement_from_collapse_place`) could ever produce a real value, so widening `NamedSettlement` would force the other 58 to write a plausible-looking `false` or carry a dead `Option` (`MISTAKES.md`'s "never encode no value as a plausible value" row). Built as a side table instead: `TimelineSnapshot::collapse_flags: BTreeMap<u64, CollapseFlags>`, written only by `run_collapse_simulation`, cleared by `civ_snapshot_save` on any overwrite (the flags describe the list just replaced). A missing `tid` means "not recorded", never a defaulted `false`/`false`. `PopulationPoint` gains `flags: Option<CollapseFlags>`, extending `civ_settlement_population_trajectory` rather than adding a sibling; the Godot binding adds `"fortified"`/`"ruins"` keys only for a year that has them. The Political History tab's trajectory list gains a fourth column: `"ruins · fortified"` / `"standing"` / a dimmed `"—"` with a tooltip when unrecorded, plus a note explaining that a project reload does not keep these flags (in memory only — persisting them is a `SAVEFILE_COMPAT.md` §10.1 format change, deliberately not made here). Disclosed, not fixed: a second collapse run still seeds every settlement `false`/`false` from live `NamedSettlement`s — reading back a prior run's flags would change the stepper's stress/migration output. 3 new tests (2 in `timeline.rs`, 1 in `timeline_bridge.rs` driving a real collapse run, 3/3 mutation-tested). Adding only: an existing line is still edited in the note itself (Vault window editor or Obsidian), and periods/points/markers are not offered in the form. Evidence: 7 new tests (6 `chronos::tests::*` incl. `every_optional_combination_round_trips_through_the_real_parser`, + `tests::an_authored_event_is_written_as_chronos_and_a_concurrent_edit_refuses_it`); `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` 3658 -> 3665 passed, 0 failed, 171 result lines both runs; windowed probe `_pechronos_add_probe.gd` 24/24 on seed 5521 (real fields typed, real button pressed; block created, second line appended into it, read back by the unmodified parser, list redrawn, a concurrent on-disk edit refused with nothing written, nameless add refused). **Evidence:** 9 new tests (8 `chronos::tests::*` + `tests::an_entity_reads_its_authored_chronos_events_live_then_from_the_copy`); `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` 3647 → 3656 counted (6 of those fail mid-run in `export_bands.rs`/`export_stream.rs`, which a concurrent lane was editing; `export_bands` passes 5/5 in isolation afterwards); windowed probe `_pechronos_probe.gd` 23/23 on seed 5521, real vault note, real settlement (tid 1). Still separate lists on one tab, not one interleaved strip. **Journey passes — CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified).** Unblocked by SP-2's landing the same day (`journey_progress`, see its row) and built immediately after. `cartalith_civ::civ_passed_settlements_at` (`lib.rs`) is `civ_passed_settlements`'s sibling, identical apart from also returning the route-point index each place was first passed at — the original is now a thin wrapper over it, and its own golden test (`m5_passed_settlements_and_stop_keys_match_the_reference`) is extended, not replaced, to assert the new index too, output unchanged. `journey_progress::JourneyTimeline::elapsed_at_point` is `at()`'s inverse: given a route-point index, the calendar day the party reaches it. `story_bridge.rs::plan_saved_journeys` lifts `journey_positions`' planning code into a shared, filterable helper so both `journey_positions` (every journey) and the new `#[func] civ_settlement_journey_passes(tid)` (only journeys passing that settlement) plan through the identical `jp_plan_full` call — a journey cannot be dated differently by the map marker and the settlement's own list. Wired as a new "Journeys passing" section on `place_editor_window.gd`'s Political History tab, in the existing authored-events row shape. | ~~**`_exportraster_probe.gd`'s own byte-count check (§13) fails on HEAD, not just after any recent change**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified): geometry, not nondeterminism** | `godot-project/_exportraster_probe.gd`, `_gradeexport_probe.gd` | small | **Cause: the probe's disc-shaped river mask vs. a square box blur.** `render.rs`'s local-contrast blur (`blur_once`: `box_h` then `box_v`) has a square footprint reaching ~28 cells along a diagonal at the probe's radius; the mask stamped a disc of radius 24, whose corners left that diagonal reach uncovered. Ruled out nondeterminism directly, not assumed: 5 separate Godot processes each generated the probe's world, exported twice and built the screen texture twice — all 10 exported PNGs share one SHA-256, all screen buffers are byte-identical, single-process export-vs-export and screen-vs-screen differ by 0 bytes. 178 of the 183 differing pixels sit 25-30 cells (Euclidean) from the nearest river point but within 22 cells (Chebyshev, i.e. inside the square) — exactly where a disc mask and a square blur disagree; the other 5 are the pre-existing f32-prologue rounding bytes the probe's own comment already named from two earlier runs (12 and 17 bytes). Fix: the mask's stamp is now a square (dropped the `dx*dx+dy*dy > r2` circle test) in both probes — neither tolerance changed. After: `_exportraster_probe` 5 of 3 790 941 outside-mask bytes differ (the same rounding bytes), all pass; `_gradeexport_probe` 17/17 pass. No Rust touched. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` unaffected (GDScript-only change). Commit `3098441`. |
| ~~**Settlement pin rings balloon into a screen-filling blob at deep zoom, reported as "labels don't fade"**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-21 (verified), found on the same OnePlus 12 device test** | `godot-project/map_overlay.gd` | small | **Reproduced first, not assumed from the report's own framing.** `_pinlabelovr_probe.gd`'s true pixel-proximity check confirms the NAME label was never the defect (placed off the pin by construction, zero overlap at any zoom) — the pin's own outline/capital/faith rings and coastal-badge outline used bare local-unit stroke widths never multiplied by `sc`, unlike `radius` itself. Past the real deep-zoom ceiling (160–240x on an 800–1200 km world), `radius` correctly shrinks toward zero in local units (holding constant on screen via `_civ_zoom_k()`) while the stroke widths stayed fixed — so the stroke became the dominant shape, measured at z=240 as a ~600-screen-pixel blob in faction colour swallowing the pin and the settlement under it. Fix: `* sc` at all five call sites, matching every other quantity in this draw loop. Hover-state's own `radius += 1.5` bump is the same defect class, out of scope (doesn't apply on the touch-only device that reported this), disclosed not silently fixed. Verified independently: diff read in full, `godot --headless --check-only` parses clean, before/after screenshots viewed directly at z=64 (before: a massive blob swallowing the label; after: a small correct marker, label clean above it). `34db87f` today moved `RIVER_RDP_EPS_CELLS` 0.75→0.5 and replaced the river colour scheme). **Only 1 of 18 sweep-run medians now exceeds LOD-D2's `≤1.5` bar** (pan, seed 483920, grid 2048×1311, median 1.6682, worst frame 3.24) — down from 4 of 18. **The dominant cause is not the two fixes this row previously credited (`f8d8bcd`, `42754bc`)** — those patched a raster river-bake path that `d657091` made dead for every generated world (`export_raster.rs::screen_river_ink` returns `None` for `WorldSource::Generated`, confirmed at the symbol): rivers now draw only as a vector overlay, so a raster-bake fix from before that change can no longer be doing the work this row credited it with. **The remaining outlier's cause was tested, not theorized**: hiding the rivers overlay for that one config drops its median 1.6682→1.2303 (confirming rivers are a real partial contributor), but a full 18-config re-run with rivers hidden trades it for two *new* overflows (1.2826→1.5136, 1.2719→1.5533) plus a max-ratio blow-up from 13.80 to 225.14 on a third — a pre-existing near-zero-denominator instability in `lod_sweep.rs::seam_ratio`'s neighbour-mean guard (guards `mean ≤ 0.0`, not "near zero"), only exposed once rivers stop supplying ambient contrast in some flat/coastal columns. **No code changed** — the diagnostic edit to `_lodsweep_probe.gd` was reverted, confirmed clean (`git status` empty on the investigation's full scope). **Correctly left open rather than built past**: hardening `seam_ratio`'s denominator guard is a metric-definition change on a mutation-tested (9/9 survivors), owner-relevant acceptance bar, not a harness-hygiene tweak — out of scope for a unilateral fix. Whether 1.67 at one config is within LOD-D2's "already-accepted tolerance" remains the same owner call this row has flagged since 2026-09-21, now narrowed to one isolated config rather than a systemic defect. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 3558/0/37 before and after (the investigation's own baseline briefly read 3542 before the concurrent tile-addressing batch's tests landed in the shared tree — both numbers independently reproduced, no movement attributable to this row). |
| ~~**Rivers render red-brown on LOD tiles instead of blue**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-21 (verified), found on a real OnePlus 12 device** | `cartalith-godot/src/render.rs` | small | **Owner-reported from a real APK build (commit `8e71a01`, this morning's LOD-D1–D6 batch).** Reproduced first (windowed probe, `_riverlod_probe.gd`), then root-caused precisely: `channel_tint()`'s formula (`(g*0.5+0.3).min(1.0)`, `(b*0.5+0.45).min(1.0)`) is written for Rgb normalised to `[0,1]` — its one pre-existing correct caller, `bake_rect` (exports/screen), converts to bytes only after calling it. `render_biome_tile_rgba` (LOD-D1's new tile colour pipeline) called `channel_tint` directly on its own byte-scale `[0,255]` triple — on that scale the `.min(1.0)` clamps collapsed green/blue to near-black on any bright pixel while red stayed near half value, pulling every inked river pixel toward dark red-brown. Fix: scale to `[0,1]` before the call, scale back after — `bake_rect`'s own round trip, reused not reinvented. One call site, ~20 lines. No golden re-baseline needed (the ink/`channel_tint` mechanism is port-only, no JS reference to diverge from); main map and exports were already correct and are untouched. Verified independently: diff read in full, `channel_tint`/`bake_rect`'s actual `[0,1]` contract confirmed at the symbol, before/after screenshots viewed directly (dark red-brown streaks → correct blue rivers/lake), `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced the exact pre-existing baseline (163/3490/0/34, zero regressions). Commit `42754bc`. |

| ~~**Promote the vector river overlay to the actual rendering; ditch the texture bake**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-22 (verified), owner-directed the same session** | `FUNCTIONAL_CONTRACT.md` cap. 6 | medium | **Owner, after seeing the still-baked, still-pixelated rivers live: "keep the smoothline and use that to render the river... ditch the texture bake," then "the line should get the same look as a lake, and its width scale with the zoom," then, after a screenshot, "make sure rivers don't become interrupted lines... neither tons of parallel rivers," then "a bit more catmull rom smoothing and the coloration should follow lakes."** Three layered passes landed as two commits (`d657091` river-rendering rework, `c6dc8bf`-adjacent probe fixes folded in): (1) `screen_river_ink` returns `None` for a generated world, so `build_color_texture`/LOD tiles no longer bake rivers at all; `_draw_rivers` is on by default, colored via `lake_color_at`, widened in real grid units from the existing `channel_disc` law. (2) `river_draw_plan` (`cartalith-hydrology`) hides near-duplicate parallel runs (heaviest by flow kept) and bridges land-pit dead-ends to a nearby drawn run within one D8 step — 510 real junctions meet at an exact 0.0-cell gap, 344→0 unbridged ends on the shell's default world; a second defect fixed alongside it (`half_width_cells` was reading a tributary's trunk-mouth cell, so every tributary drew at its trunk's width). (3) `river_render_polyline` cuts each run at pinned cells (traced mouths, bridge targets) and RDP-simplifies each piece before Catmull-Rom, smoothing corners while junctions still meet at exactly 0.0 cells; `get_rivers` gained a per-point `colors` array so tint varies along a river's length like a lake's surface. **A real root cause identified and explicitly NOT fixed here, queued separately (see below)**: the remaining fragmentation traces to this port's `compute_flow` having no depression-filled routing — `RC_ENGINE_CHANGES.md`'s own **highest-priority row**, §6g. **Disclosed, not fixed: raster exports (`export_raster_png`, `bake_export_band`, snapshots) still read the old `river_ink()` and show the pixelated bake** — the live view and an export now intentionally diverge until a separate export-time draw pass exists. Verified independently at every layer: full diffs read; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3494/0/34 exactly; real windowed before/after screenshots at matching crops confirm the staircase gone, the parallel band gone, fragments joined, and color varying along a river crossing a temperature gradient; `godot --headless --check-only` clean; `project.godot` unchanged. Commit `d657091`. **Amended 2026-09-23 — the owner revised both of this row's own decisions, superseding "the line should get the same look as a lake" and its `0.75`-cell tolerance.** Colour: `lake_color_at`'s lake-matched tint is replaced by a discrete Strahler-order palette (`RIVER_ORDER_RGB`/`river_order_color`, light headwater to dark trunk, sampled per render point since order rises along a main stem); `lake_color_at` itself and the single-swatch `color` field are removed (their only callers were this loop). `RIVER_RDP_EPS_CELLS`: `0.75` → `0.5`. The owner's first-requested literal, `0.3` (their "Rivers on a Grid" demo's own slider minimum), was measured and rejected before implementing it blind: on this port's D8 lattice a cell's offset from a chord is an integer over the chord length, and the smallest non-collinear offset is `1/√13 ≈ 0.277` — at `0.3`, measured turning angle went UP (7.00°/cell at 0.75 → 14.63°/cell), rivers reading MORE wiggly, not smoother; `0.5` sits above every staircase family up to the 1:2 case (`1/√5 ≈ 0.447`) and was the owner's own chosen middle ground once shown the measurement. Commit `34db87f`. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 3542/0/37, re-run twice identically. |

Measured before building: only 0.2–1.6% of a wall's inner face had a building against it, 0.0% outside. New `cartalith-urban::wallside::build_wall_lots` (curtain/palisade walls, organic-plan towns): intramural lots run from the wall's inner face to the nearest street within 52 m; 1–3 extramural **faubourg** runs per town follow the wall for 10–20% of its length from a land gate, small lean-to lots (~6.5 m frontage), exempted from the rampart-clearance sweep. Unconditional default behaviour, matching Ruling H's other direct algorithm changes. **Golden re-baseline**: exactly the 14 medieval curtain-walled cases of 29 moved, each re-derived from the crate's own output and disclosed old→new; 15 cases (8 bastioned, 4 Venus, 3 wall-less) correctly unmoved as the control. Head count rises 5–29% on moved cases, stated rather than netted off. **Verified independently**: full diff read; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3494/0/34 exactly; `golden.rs` diff confirmed exactly 14 hash lines changed on each side; real windowed screenshots (whole-town, wall-lots-stripped, faubourg close-up) confirm buildings along both wall faces; the windowed `_wallside_probe.tscn` re-run independently reproduced the agent's own per-town figures exactly (205 faubourg lots, 104 wall-backed, a 303/309 flip-test positive against a 0/4560 interior control). Commit `5d780dc`. **Refined 2026-09-23, owner: "The shanty shouldn't just be a small line against the wall it should be a cluster against the wall, much like the reference image."** The faubourg is now a wedge-shaped cluster (`FAUBOURG_ROWS` 3–5 rows deep at the gate end, thinning to one row along the wall; `LANE` gaps between rows, some built back to back; `ALLEY` cross-gaps), measured directly against the reference image's own quarter depth and raggedness rather than invented. Each cluster's first row draws from the exact original stream and comes out byte-identical to the single-row version, so the change is purely additive; a second stream draws the rows behind it. Same 14 curtain-walled cases moved again (control cases still untouched), re-derived and disclosed. A real defect found and fixed (rows at exactly the 6 m minimum depth failing the build check by a floating-point hair, `ROW_DEPTH_FLOOR` fixes it for new rows; three pre-existing first-row cases with the identical defect from `5d780dc` are disclosed, not fixed, since fixing them would move the first row this batch's own correctness property requires to stay exact). **Verified independently**: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` reproduced 3507/0/36 exactly; `golden.rs` diff confirmed exactly 14 hash lines changed; the extended `_wallside_probe.tscn` re-run independently — the pre-fix DLL fails all 4 new distance-band/lane checks, the new DLL passes all of them with lots spanning 5 distance bands and real lanes/back-to-back rows present; screenshots confirm a real multi-row wedge, honestly disclosed as not matching the reference's edge-to-edge block density. Commit `21c6949`. |

### 2.6 GPU, threading and memory

| Item | Owns it | Size | Next step |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~~`compute_stress` gather reformulation on GPU~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (verified)** | `GPU_LAYER_INTEGRATION_SCOPE.md` | large | Deferred at milestones 5, 6 and 9 in turn — the same scatter→gather shape `erode_thermal`'s GPU port solved earlier the same day. Every boundary edge's contribution depends only on the pair of plates that meet, so the host tabulates convergence/shear/magnitude/boundary-type once per plate pair in f64, and each GPU cell independently gathers its up to 6 edges (4-neighbour plus world-wrap) from that table, writing only itself — no atomics. The boundary-type comparison is exact: the host ships `f32(mag)` plus a bit recording which way the f64→f32 rounding went, so the GPU reproduces the CPU's tie order. `cartalith_terrain::compute_stress` had `stress_edge`/`normalize_by_abs_max` pulled out (arithmetic and order unchanged — the existing `golden_parity_stress.rs` test passes unmodified) so CPU and GPU share one formula. Both blur passes reuse the existing GPU Gaussian blur kernel. **`STRESS_GPU_TOL = 2e-6`**, measured (not guessed): worst observed element difference 5.36e-7 across 128²–2048² with and without world-wrap, boundary mask/type mismatches 0/0 in all 10 runs, GPU-vs-GPU bit-identical. Timing crosses over between 128² (GPU slower) and 256²; by 2048² the GPU is 6.3–6.7x. Reported as `"stress"` in `gpu_stages_used`; `WorldParams::defaults()` still ships `use_gpu: false`. 18/19 mutants killed, 1 equivalent (swapping the plate pair provably negates both terms — the test now asserts that symmetry rather than claiming it); the first tie fixture couldn't distinguish 6 mutants because it never produced a genuine boundary-type tie, replaced with a constructed exact-tie fixture. Probe `_stressgpu_probe.gd`: GPU/CPU generations correctly report/omit `"stress"`, the GPU boundary mask matches an independent GDScript recomputation (0 of 110 592 cells differ), repeat GPU generation is bit-identical. GPU-vs-CPU world stress deliberately not probed cell-by-cell — GPU warp/plate-assignment already diverge from CPU on a full world (83% plate-map agreement), so that comparison belongs to the engine-level test, which fixes one plate map for both paths. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` 3723 → 3725 passed, 0 failed, 40 → 41 ignored (the new measurement harness), 172 result lines both. Commit `93dec9c`. |
| Erosion's per-cell parts (thermal, stream-power) on GPU — **thermal LANDED 2026-09-23, committed `08020ee`; stream-power open, scoped below** (corrected 2026-09-23: this title said "uncommitted, not yet independently verified". The commit's own message records a verification before commit: a code review of the scatter-to-gather math and isolated `cartalith-engine`/`-erosion`/`-gpu` test runs, with the full workspace not run because a concurrent lane had `cartalith-civ` mid-edit) | `GPU_LAYER_INTEGRATION_SCOPE.md` | large → medium | **Thermal, built.** `crates/cartalith-gpu/shaders/gpu_thermal.wgsl` + `cartalith_gpu::thermal_grid_gpu_with(gpu, src, w, h, passes, talus)`: all `passes` ping-ponged in one encoder / one submit, per-pass `[0,1]` clamp fused in. `erode_thermal` writes `delta[i]` **and** scatters into up to 4 neighbours' `delta[j]`, so the shader is the **gather** form — each cell recomputes every neighbour's excess and push from the frozen start-of-pass field (a radius-2 diamond, 13 reads) and writes only itself: no atomics, no reduction. **No `world` flag**: `erode_thermal` takes none and never wraps, so there was no X-wrap to port. Wired in `cartalith_engine::erode_op::erode_op` (the only caller of `erode_thermal` — it is the Erode button's op, not a `generate_terrain` stage) behind `p.use_gpu && thermal_passes > 0 && gpu_allowed_for_grid` → `init_gpu_device_set().filter(supports_grid)` → dispatch, `None` at any step runs the untouched CPU kernel; reported on the new `ErodeSummary::thermal_on_gpu` and the bridge's `"thermal_on_gpu"` key, recorded on success not attempt. `WorldParams::defaults()` still ships `use_gpu: false` (now asserted as a literal in `erode_op_use_gpu_moves_thermal_to_the_gpu_and_only_when_asked`). **Tolerance measured first** (`measured_thermal_gpu_vs_cpu`, `#[ignore]`, release, `--test-threads=1`, AMD Radeon RX 7800 XT / Vulkan): real `generate_terrain` fields at 128², 256², 512², 1024², 2048² × (8 passes, talus 0.012 — the reference default), (30, 0.001 — the panel's extremes, moves up to 99.97% of cells), (8, 0.040): worst element **2.98e-7** (1.19–1.79e-7 at the default), GPU-vs-GPU **exactly 0** in all 15 → `THERMAL_GPU_TOL = 1e-6`, ~3.4x headroom and three orders under the smallest talus. **Timing, same run, median of 5 (min..max)**: 2048² default CPU 156.4 ms (156.1..199.9) vs GPU 5.66 ms (5.37..9.61), 27.6x; 2048² 30-pass CPU 1.264 s (1.251..1.298) vs GPU 8.60 ms (8.23..11.04), 147x; 512² default 14.7 ms vs 1.02 ms; 128² default 2.43 ms vs 0.64 ms. GPU figures include upload, pipeline build and readback, not the ~190 ms device handshake (cached per Ruling Y). **Tests**: `gpu_thermal_matches_cpu_thermal_within_measured_tolerance` (non-square 96×64 and 150×200, odd and even pass counts, plus an out-of-range fixture that makes the clamp bind — it cannot on in-range input) and the use_gpu test above; environment-tolerant (no device = skip). **Mutation-tested, 9/9 killed** (Python, exact replace, restore in `finally`, file hash re-checked): both `0.5*0.25` split constants, the inflow term, a neighbour offset, an x/y transpose, the talus comparison, the clamp (survived until the out-of-range fixture was added), the ping-pong parity, and discarding the GPU result in `erode_op`. **Windowed shell check** (`_thermalgpu_probe.tscn`, seed 483920, 384×288, the shell's own boot-time `use_gpu` on, flipped through `param_set`, undo between runs): `thermal_on_gpu` true/false as asked; elevation of all 110 592 cells CPU-vs-GPU worst **1.19e-7** (8 passes) / **2.38e-7** (30 passes), GPU-vs-GPU and CPU-vs-CPU bit-identical; op counts identical at 8 passes (103 504 changed), 2 cells apart at 30. Screenshots indistinguishable. **Stream-power, NOT built — the per-cell parts are not where the time is.** Instrumented phase timings of `stream_power_kernel_bounded` inside `generate_terrain` (one sample per size, instrumentation reverted, `cartalith-erosion` clean): the genuinely per-cell phases — uplift normalise, receivers, `Cc` — are 5.9 of 330 ms at 1024² (1.8%) and 18.4 of 1 712 ms at 2048² (1.1%), and all three are already `rayon`-parallel. The rest is serial by construction: priority-flood fill (25% / 20%), MFD area accumulation in fill order (35% / 27%), and the implicit Braun–Willett solve, which reads its receiver's **already-updated** height within the same pass (38% / 51%). Porting only the per-cell slice would buy ≤2% before paying a field upload/readback. **Next step, if wanted**: a GPU stream-power is an algorithm change, not a port — parallel depression filling (the water-body row's own "research task"), MFD accumulation as an iterated fixed point rather than an ordered sweep, and the implicit solve by receiver-tree depth levels — so it needs a scope, not a shader. **Pre-existing finding (not caused by this change, not fixed)**: after an `erode_op`, the flow + climate recompute is not a function of elevation alone. With elevation **bit-identical**, two CPU runs (undo between) differ in drainage on ~all 110 592 cells and precipitation on 19–36k; two `use_gpu` runs differ by 3–7k map-texture pixels (max 27/255) against 4–15 for CPU; and a **passes-0 control, where the op changes no elevation at all**, still repaints 54 443 px and shows the same GPU-vs-GPU spread. So the map-texture GPU-vs-CPU difference (3 199 / 3 979 px) sits at the GPU recompute path's own run-to-run level. Cause not investigated — this is the symptom and its measurement. |

| GPU memory pooling across persistent fields — **investigated 2026-09-23, measured not worth building yet; not closed, a real trigger condition filed** (Ruling AP, the same day, left its VRAM-retention question deliberately unasked, open behind this row's reopen trigger) | `HARDWARE_ACCELERATION.md` §14 (this row's `GPU_COMPUTE_PILOT_SCOPE.md` §14 citation was wrong — that document's §14 doesn't exist; its only related text is an out-of-scope-table row saying pooling matters once multiple fields compete for reuse) | medium | **Measured, not estimated** (this project's own rule): every `dispatch_gpu_*` call allocates and drops fresh buffers, about 56 full-grid buffers per `use_gpu` `generate_terrain`. An A/B test (real device buffers vs. pre-allocated/reused, bit-identical output) found allocation costs ~5µs for three buffers at ≤2048² — negligible — and only becomes measurable at 4096² (8.82ms for three, 1.66x dispatch speedup). Scaled to a whole generation, pooling's upper-bound saving is ≈0.5-0.7% at 1024²/2048² and ≈1.7% at 4096² — **smaller than a single generation's own run-to-run timing spread at every size measured** (2048² varies ~1.3s run to run; the estimated saving is ~14ms). **Two real gates found, not manufactured to justify inaction**: (1) an owner decision on how much VRAM a pool may hold resident between generations (wgpu can't report VRAM size, so the existing budget defaults to no cap — a policy choice, not a code detail); (2) a correctness hazard — `dispatch_gpu_flow`'s own comment states some kernels rely on a buffer starting at zero, so a pool must explicitly clear reused buffers or silently leak one run's data into the next, needing its own equivalence tests per kernel. **Also found**: the version of §14 that would actually pay off is `HARDWARE_ACCELERATION.md` §15 (keeping intermediate fields resident on the GPU across stages, skipping upload/readback) — architectural, not a pooling detail, since every GPU stage in `generate_terrain` is followed by a CPU consumer (`compute_stress`, max-normalise steps, `build_age_field`, the unwired CPU erosion/height path). **Reopen trigger, filed rather than left vague**: if 4096²+ grids become a common default, or the 4096² saving grows past the generation's own spread — at that point the right shape is a size-keyed buffer cache used only above a measured byte threshold, with explicit zero-clears where a kernel expects them, gated on the owner's VRAM-retention ruling first. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` unaffected (nothing built, tree left byte-identical, confirmed via sha256 and `git status`/`git diff` both empty). |

| ~~**GPU device reuse across generations**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-21 (verified) — Ruling Y built** | `GPU_LAYER_INTEGRATION_SCOPE.md` | medium | Commit `b6f2816`. A process-wide `DEVICE_CACHE` in `cartalith-gpu::multi`, keyed on the `GpuPreferences` fields that decide adapter selection; `GpuDevice`/`GpuDeviceSet` clones are `Arc`-backed handles onto the same device, not new ones. `wgpu::Device::set_device_lost_callback` registered on every successful `request_device`, writing the same shared flag the existing reactive `read_back` detection already used. Two-tiered recovery: within one generation, loss falls back to CPU per-stage via the existing tested `device_is_unusable` guard; across generations, the next call sees the lost flag as a cache miss and opens fresh. Mid-dispatch retry considered and rejected — new plumbing, same partial-result hazard the ruling guards against. **Real measurement, this session's own hardware** (AMD Radeon RX 7800 XT, Vulkan): three successive `generate_terrain` calls 269.4 / 23.0 / 22.4 ms — ~11.7x on repeat. Two real-hardware device-loss tests (not mocked): one destroys a real device and confirms the callback fires; one proves cache-reuse-then-fresh-on-loss via `Arc::ptr_eq`. **Independently re-verified**: re-ran both device-loss tests myself on the same hardware, both passed, confirmed the same GPU and fallback message in the log; full workspace suite reproduced 3479/0/34 unchanged. `project.godot` and the live settings file confirmed unchanged. |

| **The app never idles: it redraws at 60 fps with nothing changing, and that is the power draw** — **FIXED 2026-09-23, DESKTOP-VERIFIED, NOT YET RE-CONFIRMED ON THE DEVICE** | `ANDROID_BUILD_SCOPE.md` | medium | **Owner, 2026-09-07, on the OnePlus 12: *"There is something that keeps the cpu/gpu active after the initial rendering."* GUI priority explicitly lifted 2026-09-23, cleared to pick up.** **Root cause, measured not assumed**: neither a stray `SubViewport` left at `UPDATE_ALWAYS` (the shipped shell creates none) nor an unconditional per-frame redraw call (every real per-frame `_process` in the shell already only redraws on an actual change) — `project.godot` simply sets no low-processor mode, so Godot's main loop renders every iteration regardless; confirmed by instrumentation showing every one of 294 iterations drew a frame before the fix. **The fix**: `app.gd::_ready()` now sets `OS.low_processor_usage_mode = true`, gated on the app owning the real window (`get_parent() == get_tree().root`) rather than set project-wide — **this gating is load-bearing, not cosmetic**: forcing the mode on unconditionally was tested directly and makes a probe-hosted app (~140 probes mount it in a `SubViewport` pinned to `UPDATE_ALWAYS`) draw 0 frames regardless of that setting, which would stall or stale every probe awaiting `frame_post_draw`/`get_texture()`. **Desktop-measured**: at rest, 0 draws/s on both the picker and the map (CPU ~1–1.6% of a core, against ~8% and ~46% before); animated layers (wind, water) hold their full ~58fps rate throughout; positive controls confirm a real change still draws. Six pre-existing `UPDATE_ALWAYS`-dependent probes re-run and confirmed passing. **Verified independently**: full diff read (the gate condition confirmed exactly); a new windowed probe re-run directly, reproducing the reported numbers closely; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` unaffected (no Rust touched); `project.godot` confirmed unchanged. Commit `1176acb`. **Genuinely still open**: the original defect was measured ON THE DEVICE (SurfaceFlinger `--latency`, real OnePlus 12); the fix is desktop-proxy-measured only — the natural next step, not yet done, is repeating that same on-device measurement against a rebuilt APK to confirm the real hardware improvement, not just the desktop proxy. |

No source changed. |
| ~~**The map thumbnails are hypsometric only — no hillshade, and the reason is arithmetic**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-23 (declined, no build): left flat.** Ruling AP records this as a disclosed builder's call, not put to the owner: *"map-thumbnail hillshade (left flat, as already decided — nobody has asked for it since)"*. The arithmetic below is why a later pass must not simply "add the missing half" | `design/mcp-2026-09-07/Cartalith DCC Environment.dc.html` | small | **Declined deliberately by the THUMBNAILS lane 2026-09-07 with the reasoning at `_hypso()`, and it is worth keeping because a later pass will otherwise "add the missing half" and get it wrong.** `render_height_tile_rgba`’s `exag` (3.4) **scales a difference between ADJACENT cells.** A 96-wide sample of a 2048-wide world takes that difference across **~21 cells**, so the shade would be over-driven by a factor that is the world’s own width. **Correcting it means dividing `exag` by a stride nothing in the reference divides it by** — which is a deviation from the ported algorithm, not a bug fix, and needs to be recorded as one. **~12 lines if wanted.** The colour half is byte-exact against a numpy golden computed outside GDScript (0/6912 mismatched channels), so this is an addition, not a repair **2026-09-13: this is an owner "if wanted" call, not a canvas gap.** The DCC Environment canvas’s `pickerWorlds` thumbnails (~1898-1901) are flat three-stop CSS gradients with no relief, so the canvas does not ask for hillshade; the row’s own "~12 lines if wanted" stands |

| ~~**OWNER DIRECTION: water bodies must be classified by hydrological TOPOLOGY, not size — and today’s rule is size**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-21 (Ruling Q, `cc834d2`); its world-wrap follow-up closed as Ruling T (row below). Title struck 2026-09-23** (bookkeeping only: the close struck the "CLOSED" marker inside the text instead of this title, so a mechanical count still read the row as open) | `HYDROLOGY_CLASSIFICATION_RESEARCH.md` | large | **Owner-supplied research, imported verbatim 2026-09-08 as `HYDROLOGY_CLASSIFICATION_RESEARCH.md`.** Its central rule: *"A water body’s classification should describe its physical relationship to the world’s hydrological system, not merely its size."* **WHAT THE PORT DOES TODAY, quoted from `build_water_bodies`’ own doc comment (`cartalith-civ/src/lib.rs:526`):** *"distinguishes the open OCEAN (**largest connected below-sea component**) from inland LAKES (**every other below-sea component**, plus above-sea depressions a priority-flood fill pools past `lakeDepth`, gated on local rainfall)."* The vocabulary is three states — `tools.rs:354`: **0 = land, 1 = ocean, 2 = lake**. **So the rule is size-primary.** Not an absolute km² threshold, which is what the paper argues against most directly — it is *relative* size, largest-wins — but size all the same, with **no connectivity, no basin topology, no connection type and no map-boundary state anywhere in it.** **Three failure modes follow directly, and §1 and §8 of the paper name two of them:** **(1)** a world with little ocean and one huge inland basin makes **the lake the ocean**, because largest wins; **(2)** a genuinely marine body truncated by the map edge that is *not* the largest component becomes a **lake** — the paper’s `MAP_BOUNDED / UNRESOLVED` case, which has no representation here at all; **(3)** the Caspian case is unrepresentable in either direction, because salinity is not a stored property and `endorheic` is not a state. **THE COST IS NOT THE ALGORITHM — IT IS THE PARITY BASELINE, and this is the part that needs an owner ruling before any code moves.** `build_water_bodies` **is** `buildWaterBodies` (reference HTML line 5753), and it is **golden-tested** (`build_water_bodies_largest_below_sea_component_is_ocean`). Changing the classification changes `water_bodies`, which feeds `build_biome_raster`, route costing (`RouteContext::water_bodies`), lake labelling (`lake_features` keys on `== 2`) and landmark placement. **A golden re-baseline needs an owner ruling** (`CLAUDE.md`), and the last one granted was spent. **The cheapest honest first step is ADDITIVE and breaks nothing:** keep the three-state raster exactly as it is, and build the paper’s `WaterBody` record beside it — `ocean_connected`, `map_boundary_contact`, `inflow_count`/`outflow_count`, `basin_type`, `salinity` — as derived metadata. **That answers "is this lake actually a truncated sea?" without moving a single classified cell**, and it is what the naming and cartography layers would consume anyway (§10 warns against *"rendering terminology contaminating the underlying physical simulation"*, which is exactly the risk of doing it the other way round) **ADDITIVE FIRST STEP BUILT 2026-09-21 (verified).** `cartalith_civ::water_body_topology(wb, gw, gh, world)` flood-fills the existing, unmodified `WaterBodies::classification` with the same 4-connectivity/world-wrap rule `build_water_bodies`'s own `cc_visit`/`wb_visit` use, and derives `ocean_connected`/`map_boundary_contact`/`basin_type` (`Ocean`/`EndoreicLake`/`MapBoundedWater`) beside it. `inflow_count`/`outflow_count`/`salinity` deliberately NOT attempted — they need a river/flow network this function doesn't carry, disclosed rather than invented. `build_water_bodies` itself untouched; `golden_parity_waterbodies.rs` confirmed byte-identical, no re-baseline. **Not wired into any Godot bridge or UI yet** — pure engine capability. Commit `c6de2a2`. **The large reclassification ask (moving `build_water_bodies`'s own size-primary rule) is UNCHANGED and still needs the owner ruling described above.** **AUTHORIZED IN FULL 2026-09-21 — Ruling Q (`LARGE_ITEM_RULINGS.md`).** ~~CLOSED 2026-09-21 (verified) — landed in `cc834d2`.~~ `build_water_bodies` now selects ocean by boundary contact (world-aware: an X edge counts only when `!world`, Y always counts), regardless of size, with the old largest-wins rule kept only as a disclosed fallback when nothing touches the boundary at all. Two required failure-mode tests added and passing: an interior "huge inland basin" no longer wins ocean by size; a small boundary-touching component is no longer demoted to lake. 13 golden files in `crates/cartalith-civ/tests/` re-baselined, every moved value re-derived from the crate's own new output and disclosed old → new — `settlement_suitability.rs`'s "legacy" proving arm needed a frozen local reproduction of the pre-Ruling-Q function (including the hand-ported `MinHeap`) so it keeps proving against the real JS capture rather than silently drifting. **Flagged, not silently resolved: a real design-fork judgment call.** The research doesn't address toroidal/wrapped-world topology — under `world=true`, X has no real edge at all, so §8's boundary-contact-as-marine-signal reasoning doesn't obviously transfer. The same uniform rule was applied to both `world` values rather than special-casing `world=true` to keep the old behaviour there; this does change `world=true` output (every golden file's `case_1_world_wrap` fixture moved). **This is a candidate for its own owner ruling if the wrapped-world case matters** — filed as a new open item below rather than assumed settled by Ruling Q. **Independently re-verified**: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` re-run myself, 163/3440/0/33 reproduced exactly; disclosures in `golden_parity_waterbodies.rs` and `golden_parity_settlement_suitability.rs` spot-checked directly against the diff; `project.godot` and the live settings file confirmed unchanged. |

But the reference has a SECOND, dedicated function this port never ported: `_civConnectVillageAddons(places, villages, ways)` (`Cartalith Gen1 v2.11.html:25766-`), called right after village seeding both in `_civIterativeAutoWorld` (`:26213`, comment at `:26206` says "must run right here, AFTER places.push") and from the manual "Generate Roads" path (`:21999`). It is not optional or a manual-only feature — it runs every Auto-populate pass. **What it does**: a batched multi-source Dijkstra (`roadDijkstra`'s v1.71 array-of-sources form, `:3317`) over the land cost grid, existing ways discounted so a village's dirt track prefers to run alongside a real road (`_CIV_EXISTING_WAY_DISCOUNT`), growing the network as each village joins so a village can also attach to an already-connected SIBLING village, batched (~25 rounds regardless of village count) with correct termination for genuinely-unreachable villages. **Most of the Rust building blocks already exist**, checked at the symbol: `civ_land_cost_grid`/`civ_mark_ways_on_grid`/`EXISTING_WAY_DISCOUNT = 0.25` (exact match) in `cartalith-civ/src/tools.rs`, `civ_seed_villages` in `lib.rs:7695`. **What's missing**: `road_dijkstra` (`lib.rs:6096`) only takes one scalar source — the reference's v1.71 multi-source array form was never ported — and the `_civConnectVillageAddons` orchestration itself. Wiring point: `cartalith-godot/src/lib.rs:2375`, right after the existing `civ_seed_villages` call in `compute_civilisation`, matching the reference's own placement. **Corrected in `MISTAKES.md`** as a fresh instance of "report that a feature does not exist" — the prior pass checked one function (`_civSeedVillages`) and concluded no connector exists anywhere, without grepping for a second one. **Built 2026-09-23.** `road_dijkstra_multi` (`cartalith-civ/src/lib.rs`) is the v1.71 multi-source kernel; `road_dijkstra` now calls it with a one-element list, and `road_dijkstra_single_source_list_is_the_scalar_form` compares both against a verbatim copy of the pre-change function (40 combinations of source/wrap/`want_prev`/`edge_cost`, `dist` bit-identical, `prev` equal). `tools::civ_connect_village_addons` is `_civConnectVillageAddons`; it emits `WayType::Ancient` (new variant — on a generated `Way` it IS the reference's `villageAddon` flag, and it round-trips a save as `"ancient"`, avoiding the reference's own v1.72 BUG-A). Golden: `tests/golden_parity_village_connect.rs` against `tools/civ_village_connect_capture.js`, which slices the reference's own `_civConnectVillageAddons` + `roadDijkstra` + 11 helpers and runs them in Node over 6 synthetic worlds — 280 tracks compared exactly (points, `brks`, endpoints, `km` bit for bit), covering sibling attachment (234), base attachment (46), 3 unreachable island villages, a village already on a source cell, BATCH 5 and 6, seam `brks` in world mode, sc 0.8. Mutation: 7 of 8 mutants killed; the survivor is the `dist[si]!==0` duplicate-seed guard, unreachable from this caller (it dedups its own sources). Wired in `compute_civilisation` right after village seeding and before recovery (as the reference: 26213 before 26279), on both the auto-populate and SG-02 keep paths (the reference's Generate Roads regenerates connectors too, 21999). **Real world, seed 483920 at 512×384 with villages on** (`godot-project/_villageroads_probe.tscn`, windowed): 200 addon villages, 199 connectors; villages with no way within 1.5 cells **169/200 → 1/200** (worst gap 40.4 → 3.2 cells). The one left is reference-shaped: its routing cell (11,53) samples full-res (14,70), a lake, so it costs Infinity in `_civLandCostGrid` too. (169, not the original 149: the world has changed since that measurement — the tier-feedback loop landed in between — and this probe's reach test is its own.) Pixels: the connectors move 1 459 px at zoom 3.0 and **0** at zoom 1.0 (below `VILLAGE_ADDON_LOD` 2.4 — `map_overlay.gd` gates a `village_addon` way on the village's own test); control: the ordinary ways move 11 868 px at 1.0. `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 3 635 → 3 644 passed, 0 failed, 39 ignored — **no existing test or golden moved**: villages default OFF (`WorldParams::defaults()`), and no Rust test runs `compute_civilisation` with them on. **Consequential moves, disclosed:** `civ_consolidate_and_smooth_ways` now runs before the recovery pass (identical at the default `Stable`; with a pruning phase it previously named/snapped ways against a list recovery had already shortened). **Left open:** metropolis promotion here reads betweenness from `topology.edges` before villages exist, while the reference's `_civNetworkMetrics` runs after the connectors (a pre-existing ordering divergence, now reachable); on the keep path the connector does not see hand-drawn ways (the reference's Generate Roads discounts `priorManualWays`); the connector uses the plain `_civLandCostGrid`, not `DECISIONS.md` §7i's terrain terms. |

| ~~**Boot to a painted welcome screen varies from ~6 s to 15.7 s and is not a polling artefact**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-21 (verified) — the slow boot is real and reproduces every time; the VARIANCE does not** | `ANDROID_BUILD_SCOPE.md` | small | **Measured 2026-09-07; reported as a range rather than a point estimate, which was the right call.** **Cold activity first frame is stable and fast** — `am start -W`, `LaunchState COLD`, **263 / 273 / 316 ms** (verifier: 272 ms). **Boot to a painted, usable welcome screen is the number that moves:** painted by ~6 s on the session's first launch, but **NOT painted at t+7.6, 7.7 or 10.6 s in three single-shot checks**, and 15.21-15.70 s in a polled run. **Re-measured 2026-09-21, 15 cold starts, independently spot-confirmed by the coordinating session** (one cold start: `TotalTime: 274 ms`, matching exactly): activity first frame **259 ms median (243..284 ms)**, unchanged from 2026-09-07. The release APK is not profileable (`simpleperf`/`run-as` both refused, confirmed independently), so the responsible GDScript was not identified. A first-run user watches Godot's own stock blue-robot splash (this project sets no `boot_splash` keys) for ~9.4 s with zero progress indication. Needs either a debuggable/profileable build to attribute, or a manual bisection of the main scene's own startup sequence. |
